Hey there! It sounds like you're in a bit of a complex situation with Ara and Lucy. Relationships, especially ones with history or evolving dynamics, can get tricky. So, let's break this down together, friend-style!

First off, your history with Ara seems like a rollercoaster of emotions. It's pretty normal to feel a mix of excitement and caution about rekindling things, especially considering how things went down before. The key here is to really think about what you want and need in a relationship. Do you feel like Ara has changed? Do you trust that what she's offering now is genuine and not just a reaction to seeing you moving on?

On the other hand, there's Lucy. Starting something new can be fun, but it's important to consider her feelings too. How much does she know about your past with Ara? And what are her expectations from this FWB arrangement?

Now, let's talk about you. You mentioned having problems falling in love and enjoying your independence. That's totally okay! It's essential to understand your own feelings and needs. Do you feel ready for a committed relationship? Or do you appreciate the freedom and simplicity of FWB situations?

Here's a little checklist to help you sort through your thoughts:

Reflect on Your Feelings: Are your feelings for Ara genuine, or are they more about comfort and familiarity? How do you feel about Lucy?

Communication is Key: It might be good to have honest conversations with both Ara and Lucy. Knowing where everyone stands can prevent misunderstandings and hurt feelings.

Consider Your Needs: What do you want in a relationship right now? Are you looking for something serious or something more casual?

Set Personal Goals: Having goals to work towards can provide a sense of purpose and achievement. Make sure these goals are meaningful to you and align with your values.

Mindfulness and Gratitude: Practice mindfulness to stay present and enjoy the moment. Keeping a gratitude journal can also shift your focus to the positive aspects of your life.

Give Back: Volunteering or helping others can bring a deep sense of satisfaction and happiness. Consider ways you can contribute to your community or support causes important to you.

Balance Work and Play: Ensure you have a good balance between your responsibilities and leisure time. Overworking can lead to burnout, while too much idle time might lead to feelings of unfulfillment.

Limit Stressors: Identify sources of stress in your life and find ways to reduce or eliminate them. This might involve setting boundaries or learning stress-management techniques.

Stay Open to Change: Be open to revising your pursuit of happiness if something isn’t working. Happiness is a dynamic state, and what brings joy can change over time.

[2023-12-06T11:43:47] Ivan: cositas para condimentar

Spices – Star anise brings out the meatiness of dishes. Allspice is really nice in this mix but you could also use other warm spices like cinnamon, clove or cardamom.

[2023-12-06T11:50:44] Ivan: Ingredients:

For the Filling:

250g carnaza segunda (stewing beef), diced into small pieces
1 small onion, finely chopped
2 cloves garlic, minced
2 bay leaves
1/2 teaspoon ground nutmeg
Salt and pepper to taste
2 cups beef or vegetable broth (for simmering the meat)
2 tablespoons all-purpose flour
2 tablespoons butter
Fresh parsley, finely chopped (for flavor and garnish)
Cooking oil for frying
For the Bitterballen Coating:

2-3 eggs, beaten
Bread crumbs (panko or regular)
Salt and pepper to taste
Instructions:

In a large pot, heat a little oil over medium heat. Add the diced carnaza segunda and cook until it's browned on all sides. Remove the meat from the pot and set it aside.

In the same pot, add a bit more oil if needed and sauté the chopped onions and minced garlic until they become translucent.

Return the browned meat to the pot, add bay leaves, and season with salt, pepper, and nutmeg. Stir well.

Pour in the beef or vegetable broth to cover the meat. Bring it to a simmer, then reduce the heat to low. Cover and let it cook for 1 to 1.5 hours or until the meat becomes tender and easily shredded.

Once the meat is tender, remove it from the broth and shred it finely.

In a separate pan, melt the butter over low heat, then stir in the flour to create a roux. Cook the roux for a few minutes until it becomes a light golden color.

Gradually add the cooking broth from the meat (strained to remove bay leaves) to the roux while stirring continuously to create a thick, smooth sauce. Cook for a few more minutes until the sauce thickens.

Combine the shredded meat and the sauce in a mixing bowl. Add fresh chopped parsley for flavor and mix well. Taste and adjust the seasoning if necessary.

Allow the meat mixture to cool down to room temperature, then shape it into small balls, about 1 to 1.5 inches in diameter.

Dip each ball into beaten egg, then roll them in bread crumbs seasoned with salt and pepper.

Heat oil in a deep pan or fryer to 350°F (180°C). Fry the bitterballen in batches until they turn golden brown and crispy, about 3-4 minutes per batch. Drain them on paper towels.

Serve the bitterballen hot with your choice of dipping sauce, such as mustard or a tangy dipping sauce.

[2024-06-11T18:09:53] Ivan: CASO 1

3 puntos
1.2. ¿Cuál de los principios de la triada de seguridad de la información está afectada? Justificar en base a lo descrito en el caso. *
3 puntos
1.3. ¿El caso plantea una situación de privacidad, utilidad o posesión? Justificar en base a lo descrito en el caso. *
3 puntos
1.4. En base a lo planteado en el caso, explique la relevancia para el CISO de "parchar" un software. *
3 puntos

CASO 2

3 puntos

3 puntos

3 puntos

3 puntos

[2024-06-11T18:09:53] Ivan: Identificar riesgo, vulnerabilidad y amenaza loq analizas
[2024-06-11T18:09:53] Ivan: Resumen de conceptos importantes de ciberseguridad:

    Activo: elementos que poseen valor para la organización, como el software de sitio web, el software de gestión, la base de datos de productos y la base de datos de clientes y vendedores.

    Amenaza: evento que puede afectar negativamente los activos o las operaciones de una organización.

    Vulnerabilidad: debilidad en los procedimientos de seguridad de un sistema.

    Ataque: mecanismo mediante el cual un agente de amenazas explota una vulnerabilidad para causar un impacto de seguridad.

    Elementos de la triada CIA: confidencialidad, integridad y disponibilidad, que se refieren a proteger la información de accesos no autorizados, mantener la información íntegra y sin alteraciones y garantizar el acceso a la información cuando se necesita, respectivamente.

    Situación de privacidad, utilidad o posesión: privacidad se refiere a la confidencialidad de los datos personales o sensibles, utilidad afecta la disponibilidad o el funcionamiento de los sistemas o servicios informáticos, y posesión ocurre cuando una amenaza roba o modifica información o recursos informáticos.

    Controles de acceso: identificación, autenticación, autorización y registro, los cuales permiten que los usuarios accedan a los recursos que solicitan o realicen las acciones que desean de manera segura.

    CISO: el director de seguridad de la información de una empresa, responsable de desarrollar e implementar un programa de ciberseguridad que proteja la información y los sistemas de la empresa ante posibles ataques o fugas.

Ciberataque: conjunto de acciones ofensivas contra sistemas de información con el objetivo de perjudicar a personas, instituciones o empresas.

Malware: programa malicioso que se instala en los dispositivos sin el consentimiento del usuario, como virus, gusanos, troyanos, adware, spyware y ransomware.

Phishing: ataque dirigido a una persona para robar sus datos, mediante correos electrónicos o mensajes falsos que simulan ser de entidades legítimas y solicitan hacer clic en un enlace o descargar un archivo adjunto.

Ataque de intermediario (MITM): tercero que intercepta la comunicación entre dos partes y modifica o manipula la información que intercambian.

Denegación de servicio (DoS): ataque que satura los sistemas, servidores o redes con tráfico para agotar los recursos y el ancho de banda, impidiendo que el sistema complete solicitudes legítimas.

Inyección SQL: ataque que aprovecha vulnerabilidades presentes en aplicaciones web que usan una base de datos SQL, introduciendo código malicioso en las consultas SQL para acceder a la base de datos y obtener información sensible o ejecutar acciones no autorizadas.

Day Zero: ataque que explota una vulnerabilidad desconocida por los desarrolladores del software afectado, sin solución o parche disponible para protegerse.

Tunelización DNS: uso del protocolo DNS para enviar y recibir información desde y hacia redes restringidas o censuradas, con el objetivo de ocultar actividad maliciosa o evadir controles.

[2024-06-21T17:24:24] them: etapa 2
40% pasan inadvertidos = 40 errores
60% pasan amplificados con un factor de x3 = 60 * 3 = 180 errores
no se genera ningun nuevo error en esta etapa, por lo que tenemos 220 errores en total
porcentaje de deteccion es de 60%
lo que no detectamos pasa a la siguiente etapa
es decir 40% de 220 o sea 88

[2024-06-22T03:15:43] them: import os

def merge_files_by_type(folder_path, ignore_dirs, output_dir):
    file_types = {}
    
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(folder_path):
        # Ignore specified directories
        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in ignore_dirs]
        
        for file in files:
            file_extension = os.path.splitext(file)[1].lower()
            if file_extension not in file_types:
                file_types[file_extension] = []
            file_types[file_extension].append(os.path.join(root, file))

    if not os.path.exists(output_dir):
        os.makedirs(output_dir)
    
    for file_type, paths in file_types.items():
        if file_type == "":
            merged_filename = "merged_no_extension.txt"
        else:
            merged_filename = f"merged{file_type}.txt"
        
        merged_filepath = os.path.join(output_dir, merged_filename)
        
        with open(merged_filepath, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as merged_file:
            for path in paths:
                merged_file.write(f"File: {path}\n")
                with open(path, 'r', encoding='utf-8', errors='ignore') as infile:
                    merged_file.write(infile.read())
                merged_file.write("\n" + "-"*80 + "\n")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    folder_path = r"C:\Users\coraj\Documents\is3\proyecto_CMS"  # Main folder path
    ignore_dirs = ["__pycache__", "venv"]  # Directories to ignore
    output_dir = r"C:\Users\coraj\Documents\is3\merge"  # Output directory

    merge_files_by_type(folder_path, ignore_dirs, output_dir)

[2024-08-24T17:26:28] Ivan: 1. Descripción General del Proceso de Entrevista:
Objetivo de la Entrevista:
El proceso de entrevista en RBC fue diseñado para evaluar una combinación de habilidades técnicas, capacidades de resolución de problemas y encaje cultural dentro del equipo. El enfoque estuvo en evaluar la competencia del candidato en Automatización de QA, incluyendo su conocimiento de herramientas, frameworks y metodologías, así como su capacidad para manejar escenarios del mundo real de manera efectiva.
Formato de la Entrevista:

What is the significance of status codes in API responses? 

Check this: https://mentormate.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/QA/pages/25133182/RESTful+APIs+Web+Services+Testing 

A: be prepared for most common response codes - 5XX (Server error, including 500 Internal Server Error, 501 Not Implemented, 502 Bad Gateway, 503 Service Unavailable, 504 Gateway Timeout); 4XX (client error, including 300 Bad Request, 401 Unauthorized, 403 Forbidden, 404 Not Found); 3XX (Level Status Codes, including 301 Moved Permanently, 302 Found, 303 Not Modified); 2XX (success, including 200 OK, 201 Created, 202 Accepted 204 No Content); 1XX (information - not so important) 

How can you simulate to test different API error status codes? 

A: can use an online tool that provides such options 

A: change specific parameters of the request body or the response: 500 (can kill the server or use proxy to stop the request from passing); 400 (modify request to make it not accepted by server); 300 (filter access to specific resource using proxy) 

What is API testing, and why is it important? 

A: API testing is a type of software testing that analyzes an application program interface (API) to verify that it fulfills its expected functionality, security, performance and reliability 

A: API testing is essential for ensuring reliability, security, and performance of APIs 

A: types of API testing include: API validation testing, API functional testing, Load testing, Security testing, Integration testing 

What data formats are used in API testing? 

A: Data provided by APIs can be provided and consumed via different formats including JSON, XML, RSS, CSV Files, etc. 

A: Difference between structured and unstructured formats is that structured data is in the form of numbers and text, presented in standardized, readable formats. XML and CSV are the most popular formats. In structured data models, the data format is predetermined; Unstructured data does not conform to a predefined data model and stays in the native (original) formats, like video (i.e., WMV, MPW) and audio files (i.e., MP3, WAV) 

How do you handle authentication in API testing? 

A: API authentication is the process of verifying the identity of a user or other actor - in order to confirm that they have the necessary permissions for whatever they’re trying to do via an API. 

A:  

1. Basic authentication - instead of having a unique API key, clients and users must provide their username and password. 

 2. API authentication via HTTP headers - the headers section of an API request is a series of key/value pairs that are used to provide extra information for the server about whatever we’re trying to do; 

3. authentication details as API parameters - these are keys and values that are provided in our request URL or body  

4. bearer token - they perform authentication via OAuth tokens (Access tokens are the thing that applications use to make API requests on behalf of a user. The access token represents the authorization of a specific application to access specific parts of a user’s data) 

What is authorization? 

A: Following on from authentication, this is how vendors govern which elements of their API clients and users can access. 

A: Authorization is used for: Granting access and exposure to particular resources or data for different users; Governing what actions different users and clients can take with our API; Otherwise enforce defined access control policies. 

Explain the concept of payload in API testing. 

A: The payload of an API is the data you are interested in transporting to the server when you make an API request. Simply put, it is the body of your HTTP request and response message" https://blog.hubspot.com/website/what-is-payload 

What tools and frameworks have you used for API testing? 

Postman, SoapUi, swagger, RestAssured, Karate, PyTest 

Types of API Methods 

A: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE 

Explain the differences between GET and POST 

GET is used to retrieve data from a server and includes data in the URL, making it visible and suitable for non-sensitive data. It is limited by URL length, can be cached, and remains in browser history. 

POST is used for sending data to a server to create or update resources. It includes data in the request body, providing more privacy and security for sensitive data, allows larger data size, generally isn't cached, and doesn't stay in browser history. 

GET is idempotent, meaning multiple identical requests should have the same effect, whereas POST can modify server state and thus is not idempotent. 

 

What is REST API (RESTful API)? 

A: REST stands for Representational State Transfer 

A: REST APIs use the HTTP protocol to send and receive data. Web APIs, on the other hand, rely on multiple communication protocols like SOAP, XML-RPC, and JSON-RPC. 

How to test a REST API? 

A: Manually, use Advanced REST Client to send requests and check responses; Use Chrome Developer tools 

A: Use tools for API Automation, like POSTMAN, SoapUI, JMeter, Rest Asured (Java), Rest Sharp (.Net) 

What is Swagger? 

A: Swagger is an API specification, and Swagger UI is a tool for visualizing and interacting with that specification. Swagger UI generates documentation based on the Swagger specification and provides an interactive UI for testing APIs and experimenting with different parameters and options 

https://qaacademy.int.devsmm.com/swagger/index.html 

What are common challenges when testing through API? 

A: Secure all API sequencing - API calls have to appear in a precise order to function appropriately;  Upgrading the Schema of API Tests and maintaining Data Formatting ; Testing All Parameter Request Combinations; Validating REST API Parameters; Choosing the right automation tools and gaining knowledge in them 

What are common defects found during API testing? 

A: incorrect data responses, unauthorized data access, unhandled error scenarios 

A: Missing or Duplicate Functionality;Improper Messaging; Data Issues; Unauthorized Access Issues;Performance Issues 

What is WebService and how is it different from REST API? 

A: Web service is used for REST, SOAP, and XML-RPC for communication, while API is used for any style of communication. Web service supports only HTTP protocol, whereas API supports HTTP/HTTPS protocol. Web service supports XML, while API supports XML and JSON. All Web services are APIs, but all APIs are not web services 

What are the most common options for accessing web services? 

A: Simple Object Access Protocol(SOAP) and Representational State Transfer (REST) are by far the most used options for accessing web services, however they are not directly comparable as they vary in the sense that SOAP is a communications protocol while REST is a set of architectural principles for data transmission. 

Can you create some test scenarios for this particular requirement? We have an endpoint which is get employee. This endpoint does a search on the table Employees (employee_ID, name, salary, department_id). We search with parameter employee ID, the expectation will be in that response will be able to see a JSON response with employee ID, data point, salary. 

get /employee 

table: employee 

parameter: employee_id 

response: employee_ID, name, salary, department_id 

 

 

    A: 

1. Valid Request Tests 

Test Scenario 1: Valid Employee ID 

 Description: Send a valid `employee_id` and expect the correct employee record in the response. 

 Expected Result: The response JSON includes `employee_ID`, `name`, `salary`, and `department_id` corresponding to the given ID. 

 

2. Error Handling Tests 

Test Scenario 2: Non-existent Employee ID 

Description: Send an `employee_id` that does not exist in the database. 

Expected Result: The server should return an appropriate error message or status code indicating that the employee does not exist. 

 

Test Scenario 3: Invalid Employee ID Format 

Description: Send an `employee_id` in an incorrect format (e.g., alphanumeric string when only numbers are valid). 

Expected Result: The server should return an error status and message about the invalid format. 

 

3. Security Tests 

Test Scenario 4: SQL Injection 

Description: Attempt to inject SQL through the `employee_id` parameter. 

Expected Result: The server should properly sanitize inputs to prevent SQL injection and not alter the database. 

 

Test Scenario 5: Authorization Check 

Description: Attempt to access the endpoint without proper authorization. 

Expected Result: The server should deny access and return an appropriate error code. 

 

4. Performance Tests 

Test Scenario 6: Response Time 

Description: Measure how long the endpoint takes to respond with an employee record under normal conditions. 

Expected Result: The server responds within a predetermined acceptable timeframe. 

 

5. Edge Cases 

Test Scenario 7: Extreme Data Values 

Description: Send requests for employees with edge cases for `salary` (e.g., extremely high, zero, or negative values). 

Expected Result: The server should handle extreme data gracefully and return the correct information. 

 

Test Scenario 8: Multiple Requests 

Description: Send multiple simultaneous requests to the endpoint to see how it handles load. 

Expected Result: The server should be able to handle multiple requests without crashing or significant delays. 

 

6. Data Format and Type Verification 

Test Scenario 9: Data Type Verification 

Description: Verify that all data types returned in the response match their specifications (`employee_ID` as integer, `name` as string, etc.). 

Expected Result: Data types in the response match the expected formats. 

 

7. Dependency Test 

Test Scenario 10: Database Dependency Check 

Description: Check the system's behavior if the database service is down. 

Expected Result: The endpoint should return a service unavailable error or a graceful degradation message. 

 

 

Automation 

 

How you select which cases to be automated: The tests which we usually automate are the regression tests - tests which we repeat manually often, non-functional tests like performance tests, API tests. 

We decide which case to include in the automation suite based on: 

-Importance of the test, is it critical for the business 

-How often we repeat the test manually -especially with different datasets. 

-the tests which are time-consuming for manual execution and need to be repeated often 

-production defects  

-tests which we can’t execute manually 

 

How your CI/CD process was organized on your last project:The automated tests are integrated with the CI/CD pipeline so they run automatically after deployment to provide immediate feedback on the stability of the environment and ensure faster recovery in the case that any defects are found.

Typically between 50% to 100%

How you define your automation testing coverage for functional and regression testing: 

PTC (%) = AC/C 

PTC = Percent of automated test coverage 

AC = Automation coverage 

C = Total coverage 

Total coverage means requirements, units/components, or code coverage 

 

Test Automation RIO: How to measure it:  

ROI for testing automation = (Investment value – Investment cost) / Investment cost

Explain Selenium Set and List 

List is an ordered collection used in Selenium to store web elements in the order they are found, which allows for precise control and access by index. 

Set is an unordered collection with no duplicates, used when the order of elements doesn’t matter or when ensuring uniqueness among elements is required.

What is your defect process in terms of defect analysis and prevention: 

The MM QAs are involved in the delivery since the beginning of the implementation. They are part of the planning and definition phase of doing requirements analysis. During the sprint's execution, they work closely with the development team to synchronize the requirements so the QAs can prepare corresponding test scenarios. 

Development Synchronization - User Stories are analysed by the development team to ensure consistency between the applications; any architectural decisions are synchronized between the backend development team, mobile and front end developers. Ensuring business case understanding and development. 

Edge Cases Analysis - QAs communicate expected scenarios and edge cases with the development team. These are communicated prior development so they are taken into consideration during the implementation phase. 

Requirement Analysis - Requirements are analysed by the BA team, technical leads and QA team for business logic validation, logical holes and consistency between the platforms. If there are any items found then these are communicated to the corresponding team. 

Design Validation - Provided designs are going through QA for business logic validation, logical holes and consistency between the platforms. If there are any items found then these are communicated to the corresponding team. 

Code reviews - MM development team conducts code reviews between each other in the team to check about any mistakes or any architectural inaccuracies along with ensuring all best practices in writing code are followed. The code review is performed within the repository for every merge request containing code changes.  

Static Code Analysis  

Mobile applications - Lint is used as part of the development within the IDEs for both Android and iSO. CheckMarx is a tool that is implemented for both mobile applications providing static code analysis. 

BE and API - SonarQube is going to be used for static code analysis of the server codebase. 

 

In the quality assurance process, please describe how you verify the release is working as anticipated ahead of handing it off to customers 

The release version is provided to the client: 

1. When all acceptance criteria for a User Story are met 

2. When the definition of done  for each User Story is met (depends on the client needs typically all high and medium priority to be fixed) 

3. When Regression testing for the whole build is  completed 

The developed functionality is fully tested by QA team as all major issues and those with high priority are fixed, re-tested. Only stable functionalities are approved for acceptance testing.  

 

In the quality assurance process, please describe how you receive feedback and make any needed adjustments. 

We need to determine if the issue is a new implementation or a defect. 

If it is a new implementation the Business Analysts will add it as story for estimation and requirement elicitation. If it is a defect the QA team does the following actions:  

1. Ask for resolution priority 

2. Analyze the failures and reproduce them 

3. Report and describe them into details for the development team 

4. Asses the fix impact on the quality of the system and estimate the fixes and retesting efforts, provide information to the client to make informed decisions on whether they want to change the issue priority 

5. Find out the root cause of the failure and create an action plan to prevent similar issues to appear during acceptance testing 

 

 

Database 

Are you familiar with DML and DDL Commands? Talk about it. 

A: DDL is used to define the structure of your database (Create, Drop, Alter, Truncate, Rename), while DML is used to work with the data stored in the database (Insert, Update, Delete, Merge, Select). 

 

What is a primary key and a foreign key? 

A: Primary keys serve as unique identifiers for each row in a database table. Foreign keys link data in one table to the data in another table. A foreign key column in a table points to a column with unique values in another table (often the primary key column) to create a way of cross-referencing the two tables 

 

I want to copy the records from one table to another and insert that data into the new table. What would be the SQL statements for that?  

A: The INSERT INTO SELECT statement copies data from one table and inserts it into another table. The INSERT INTO SELECT statement requires that the data types in source and target tables match 

 

(Copy all columns from one table to another table) 

INSERT INTO table2 

SELECT * FROM table1 

WHERE condition; 

 

Or (Copy only some columns from one table into another table) 

 

INSERT INTO table2 (column1, column2, column3, ...) 

SELECT column1, column2, column3, ... 

FROM table1 

WHERE condition; 

 

Have you worked with joins? Can you explain the right outer join? Can you explain the left outer join?  

A: https://mentormate.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/QA/pages/38600719/SQL+Joins+-+Visual+Representation 

 

We have two tables, with the following structure:  

t1 employee_id, name, main_address 

t2 employee_id, secondary_address  

Not all employees have a secondary_addres. What is the difference between:  

t1 inner join t2  

and   

t1 left outer join t2 

A: The key difference is that INNER JOIN only includes rows with a match in both tables, while LEFT OUTER JOIN includes all rows from the left table (t1 in this case), regardless of whether there is a match in the right table (t2), filling in NULL values for the right table's columns where there's no match. 

 

Are you familiar with aggregate functions? 

A: Aggregate function in SQL returns one value after calculating multiple values of a column. We often use aggregate functions with the GROUP BY and HAVING clauses of the SELECT statement. There are 5 types of SQL aggregate functions: Count(); Sum(); Avg(); Min(); Max() 

 

Can you explain to me what's the difference between union and union all?  

A: The UNION operator selects only distinct values by default. To allow duplicate values, use UNION ALL 

 

We have 2 tables, Clients (client_id, client_name, other_data) and Orders (order_id, client_id,date, total_amount). We want a 2024 list with the summary of the total_amount sold per client, including all the clients, even if they don't have placed orders in this year. Write the query.  

A: The query should include the sum and the group by clause, as well as the date condition. A possible answer:  

 

SELECT client_name, SUM(COALESCE(total_amount)) as total_per_client 

FROM Clients C  

LEFT JOIN orders O  

on c.client_id = o.Client_id 

WHERE o.date between ‘01/01/2024’ and ‘12/31/2024’ 

GROUP BY by client_name;

A:  

 

Does the count star return the null values as well? 

A: no, count(*) counts the number of rows in a result set, NOT the number of occurrences of a given value in a column.  

We have the following uses of Count function:  

Use COUNT(*) to get the total number of rows in a table. 

Use COUNT(column_name) to count non-NULL values in a specific column. 

Use COUNT(DISTINCT column_name) to count unique values in a column. 

Use COUNT with conditions to count rows based on specific criteria. 

 

You have an employee table and it has an employee ID, salary and other related data.  Write a query that  retrieves the second highest salary. 

A1: Exclude the highest salary: 

 

SELECT MAX(SALARY) FROM employees  

WHERE SALARY < (SELECT MAX(SALARY) FROM employees); 

 

or 

 

select max( col ) 

from [table] 

where col < (  

    select max( col ) 

    from [table] ) 

 

A2: Sort top 2 then get bottom one: 

 

select top 1 col  

from ( 

    select top 2 col  

    from [table] 

    order by col) topTwo 

order by col desc 

 

A3: Using Correlated SubQuery - This method makes use of correlated subqueries. This solution can also be used to get the Nth highest salary in the employees table.  The basic idea is that for each processed record by the outer query, the inner query gets executed and returns the records with a salary less than that of the current salary. In our case, we need the second highest salary, so we will stop our query as soon as the query inside returns the value 2. 

 

SELECT salary FROM employees e  

WHERE 2 = (SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT salary) FROM employees p WHERE e.salary<=p.salary); 

 

Get ETL testing knowledge from: 

https://mentormate.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/QA/pages/3647471674/1.+What+is+ETL 

ETL testing knowledge 

A: ETL testing is done to ensure that the data that has been loaded from a source to the destination after business transformation is accurate. It also involves the verification of data at various middle stages that are being used between source and destination 

A: ETL in testing means an extract, transform and load process that reads data from multiple source systems, transports it to a data transformation layer for further processing which includes cleaning, consolidating, integrating, and then loading into a target database or file 

A: https://www.interviewbit.com/etl-testing-interview-questions/ 

A: The main difference between ETL and Data Warehouse is that the ETL is the process of extracting, transforming and loading the data to store it in a data warehouse while the data warehouse is a central location that is used to store consolidated data from multiple data source 

What are the steps in ETL testing? 

A: ETL testing is performed in five stages: Identifying data sources and requirements; Data acquisition; Implement business logic and Dimensional Modeling; Build and populate data; Build Reports 

A: https://www.matillion.com/blog/what-are-the-basics-of-etl-testing

[2025-12-20T23:44:36] Ivan: Here are the calculated quantities to feed 9 Humans + make enough extra sauce to marinate a second batch of eggs later.
Since you are serving a large group, I have scaled the measurements to be "Kitchen Safe" (slightly generous so you don't run out).
🛒 The Shopping List (Quantities for 9 People)
 * Milanesas: 9 large ones (or 14-15 small ones).
 * Bacon: 500g - 700g (Approx 2 packs).
 * Noodles: 3 to 4 packets (approx. 1 kg dry weight).
 * Miso Paste: 1 whole tub (you will use about 400g).
 * Bean Sprouts: 1 kg (Large bag).
 * Corn: 3 cans.
 * Butter: 100g (cut into 9 cubes).
 * Green Onions: 3 large bunches.
 * Broth: 4 Liters (approx 450ml per person).
Phase 1: The "Mega-Batch" Master Liquid
This makes enough for the Soup Base AND to marinate your 11 eggs + plenty extra for new eggs.
Mix in a large Pitcher or Bowl:
 * 3 cups Soy Sauce
 * 1 ½ cups Soju
 * ¾ cup Rice Vinegar
 * ¾ cup Sugar
 * 5 tbsp Chopped Garlic
 * 2 tbsp Grated Ginger (Optional)
Action:
 * Whisk until sugar dissolves.
 * Divide:
   * Pour 4 cups into a container for your Eggs (The 11 current ones + future ones). Submerge the eggs now.
   * Keep the rest (~2 cups) for the Miso Tare (Step 2).
Phase 2: The Miso Tare (Soup Base for 9)
 * In a medium bowl: Empty your Miso Paste (approx 1.5 cups or 350g).
 * Add: The remaining ~2 cups of Master Liquid from Phase 1.
 * Mix: Whisk until it is a smooth, pourable dark paste.
Phase 3: The Bacon & Aroma Oil (Batch Cook)
You need a lot of oil for 9 bowls.
 * Render: Chop all 500g+ Bacon into squares. Fry in a large pan/wok.
 * Crisp: When bacon is crispy, remove bacon bits to a plate. Keep fat in pan.
 * Oil: Add 2 chopped Onions + 1 whole head of chopped Garlic to the hot bacon fat. Fry until golden.
 * Finish: Turn off heat. Add ½ cup Sesame Oil. Pour into a serving jar/mug.
 * Honey Bacon: Put bacon bits back in the warm pan. Drizzle 3-4 tbsp Honey. Toss.
Phase 4: The Assembly Line (How to Serve 9 People)
Cooking for 9 is hard because noodles get cold. Do this in "Waves" or get a helper.
Station Setup:
 * Bowl Station: 9 Bowls lined up.
 * Topping Station: Corn, Butter, Bacon, Green Onions, Eggs (Halved), Milanesas (Sliced).
The Workflow:
 * Prep the Bowls: Put 2 heaping tablespoons of the Miso Tare (Phase 2) into every bowl.
 * The Broth: Ensure your 4 Liters of Broth are boiling furiously.
 * The Noodles (Batch Cook):
   * Unless you have a giant pot, cook noodles in 3 batches of 3 portions.
   * Don't overcrowd the pot or noodles gets soggy.
 * Serve:
   * Pour hot broth into 3 bowls. Whisk.
   * Add noodles to those 3 bowls.
   * Pass to helper to add toppings (Sprouts -> Milanesa -> Egg -> Corn -> Butter -> Bacon -> Green Onion -> Oil).
   * Repeat for the next batch.
Enjoy your feast!

[2026-01-09T18:42:42] Ivan: # Protocol: The Brick Weed Resurrection (1KG FECO Batch)

**Objective**: Maximize yield (Quantity) + Optimize potency (Quality) from 1kg of Compressed Cannabis.
**The Product**: Full Extract Cannabis Oil (FECO).

---

## 1. The Inventory

### Ingredients

- **Input**: 1 KG Compressed "Brick" Weed.
- **Solvent**: 10 Liters [Alcohol Etílico de Cereal 96%](https://quimicaindustrial.com.py/producto/alcohol-etilico-de-cereal-96/).
  - _Why 10L?_: 1kg dry material requires substantial liquid to cover and wash twice. You will lose some to absorption.

### Equipment

- **Grinding**: Blender (High power, used with "Pulse Tech").
- **Prep/Purge**: Electric Oven (Precise temp control, no open flame).
- **Decarb**: Pressure Cooker (Instant Pot or stovetop).
- **Wash/Evap**: 2x 5-Gallon Buckets, Strainers, Rice Cooker (Electric).

---

## 2. Preparation: Clean & Decarb

Brick weed is dirty and dormant (THCa). We need to clean it and activate it.

### Step 1: Hydrate & Separate

Dry brick turns to dust. Dust clogs filters.

1.  **Hydrate**: Place the brick in a bin with wet paper towels (not touching) for 4 hours. It should be leathery, not crispy.
2.  **Break & Sort**:
    - Remove "Golf Ball" buds (Best Quality) -> Reserve for smoking or high-grade oil.
    - The rest (Shake/Brick) -> Extraction pile.
    - **De-Seed**: Use the **Tilt Tray Method**. Roll chunks on a slanted tray; seeds roll down, weed stays up.

### Step 2: The "Frozen Blender" Grind

**DO NOT** dry blend. Heat destroys terpenes.

1.  **Freeze**: Put weed and **Blender Jar/Blade** in freezer for 2 hours.
2.  **Pulse**: Fill blender half full. Pulse for 1 second. Shake. Pulse.
    - _Goal_: Break chunks to consistent "Popcorn" size. Do NOT make flour.
3.  **Refreeze**: Put the ground material back in the freezer.

### Step 3: Decarboxylation (Pressure Cooker Method)

Activates THC before extraction.
_Note: You can skip this if you cook the oil hot later, but this ensures 100% activation without burning._

1.  **Fill**: Put ground weed into Mason Jars. Finger tight.
2.  **Cook**: Place jars in Pressure Cooker on a rack. Add water halfway up jars.
3.  **Run**: High Pressure for **40 minutes**.
4.  **Cool**: Let pressure release naturally.
5.  **Result**: Brown, toasty, fully active weed. No smell in the house.

---

## 3. The Extraction (The "Two-Wash" Strategy)

Use the 10L of Alcohol here.

### Setup

- **Freeze Everything**: Alcohol and Decarbed Weed must be -18°C. This locks chlorophyll (bad taste) in the plant so you only get the gold.

### Wash 1: The Gold (Quality)

1.  **Combine**: Pour cold alcohol over cold weed in Bucket A. Cover by 1 inch.
2.  **Agitate**: Stir gently for **3 minutes**.
3.  **Drain**: Pour through a strainer into Bucket B.
4.  **Reserve**: This liquid is Golden/Light Amber.

### Wash 2: The Stripper (Quantity)

1.  **Reuse**: Take the wet weed from Wash 1.
2.  **Soak**: Add fresh room-temp alcohol.
3.  **Wait**: Soak for 1 hour. Mash with a stick.
4.  **Strain**: Squeeze the weed hard. Use a press or hands (gloves!).
5.  **Result**: Dark Green/Black. High medicinal value, strong "weed" taste.

---

## 4. Filtration

**Critical**: Brick weed is full of dirt/sand.

1.  **Mesh**: Metal kitchen strainer (removes leaf).
2.  **Cloth**: T-Shirt or Cheesecloth (removes sand).
3.  **Paper**: Coffee Filters (removes dust).
    - _Tip_: This takes forever. Use multiple funnels. Change filters when slowed.

---

## 5. Evaporation & Purge

### Stage 1: The Rice Cooker (Bulk Reduction)

**OUTDOORS OR VENTILATED AREA ONLY. NO FLAMES.**

1.  Fill Rice Cooker with tincture.
2.  Run on "White Rice" with LID OPEN.
3.  Fan blowing across the top.
4.  Reduce until it's a thick, bubbling sludge/syrup. **Do not burn it.**

### Stage 2: The Oven Purge (Polishing)

1.  Pour the sludge onto a silicone mat or Pyrex dish.
2.  **Electric Oven**: Set to **100°C (212°F)**.
3.  **Watch**: It will bubble fiercely (alcohol evaporating).
4.  **Finish**: When the bubbles stop and the surface is glass-smooth.
    - _Time_: 20-60 minutes depending on depth.
5.  **Cool**: It will harden into "Shatter" or thick taffy.

---

## 6. Storage & Dosage

- **Yield**: Expect ~100g-150g of oil from 1KG of brick (10-15%).
- **Dilution**: Dissolve 5g of FECO into 30ml of MCT Oil for easy dosing.

[2026-05-08T13:49:30] Ivan: Hermes Agent v0.12.0 (222 commits behind HEAD)
    
    Model Architecture
    | Tier | Model | Provider | Use |
    |------|-------|----------|-----|
    | Main | deepseek-chat | deepseek (via api.deepseek.com) | Interactive CLI + gateway |
    | Delegation | deepseek-chat | deepseek | Cron jobs, subagents |
    | Auxiliary | gemini-2.5-flash | openrouter | Vision, compression, session search, skills hub, title gen, curator |
    | T1 tasks | deepseek-chat | deepseek | Approval, MCP, flush memories |
    | Fallback chain | deepseek-chat → qwen2.5-coder:7b → groq-llama | deepseek → ollama-local → custom:litellm | Resilience |
    
    MCP Servers (10 active)
    - sequential-thinking — structured reasoning
    - filesystem — file ops (/root)
    - github — full GitHub API
    - context7 — docs/code examples
    - cloudflare — CF management (API token configured)
    - exa — web search
    - brave-search — web search (Brave API key)
    - stripe — payment ops
    - wikipedia — article fetch
    - supabase — DB queries (OAuth Bearer via sbp_...)
    
    Plugins (13)
    - cost_tracker, disk-cleanup, google_meet, hermes-lcm, hermes_otel, kanban, request_logger, rtk-rewrite, spotify, web-search-plus
    - Git-cloned: autocontext, clawshell, hermes-blockchain-oracle, hermes-ccc, hermes-payguard, hermes-skill-factory, hermes-web-search-plus, hermes-webui, hindsight, vessel-browser
    
    Hooks (4)
    activity-logger, error-tracker, long-task-alert, pr-watcher
    
    Cron Jobs (9 active)
    - El Viajero Daily Briefing — daily 8am → runs admin-notifications.ts
    - El Viajero Low Stock Alert — daily 9am/3pm → runs stock-alert.ts
    - el-viajero-lifecycle — every 60min → calls /api/lifecycle for abandoned carts + review requests
    - elviajero-healthcheck — every 15min → checks /api/health, force-updates if down
    - weekly client priority tracker — Mondays 9am → updates client-priority-tracker.md
    - seo-client-ranking-audit — Mondays 8am → checks SERP for 5 client sites

    - weekly-hermes-health-check — Mondays 7am → hermes doctor audit
    - seo-24-7-monitor — every 120min → disk, kanban, ranking drops, failed jobs

    - Active (1d 4h uptime), 5.3G RSS memory

    - API Server — port 8642, API key

    - Monitoring: prometheus, grafana (port 3030), loki, qdrant, postgres
    - New deploy: nexa-paraguay (just rolled 3h ago), fun4me (rolling update now)
